                                              


Global class field theory says: the homomorphism ϕ factors through the largest abelian quotient of
G, and for every β ∈ E × the sum over all places w of E of the values of ϕ on the local reciprocity
image of β at w is 0. Take β = 2 + i, of norm 5. At a finite place w ∤ 5, β is a unit and ϕ is
unramified, so the term is 0. At the infinite place the term is 0, because C× is connected. There
remain v and v ′ . They correspond to the two square roots of −1 in Z5 . Let v be the place at which
i ≡ 7 (mod 25); indeed 72 = 49 ≡ −1. At v, β ≡ 9 (mod 25) is a unit, and

                              94 = 6561 ≡ 11      (mod 25),         λ(9) = 2.                   (III.273)

At v ′ , i ≡ −7 and β ≡ −5 (mod 25) is not a unit. Write β = 5/β̄ with β̄ = 2 − i. At v ′ , β̄ ≡ 9
(mod 25), so β = 5u with λ(u) = −λ(9) = −2. The relation is

                     0 = a1 · (−ϵ) · 2 + a2 · (−ϵ) · (−2) = −2ϵ (a1 − a2 )      in F5 .         (III.274)

Hence a1 = a2 . (The unit i gives no condition, as it must: λ(7) = 0, because 74 ≡ 1 (mod 25).)
    Step 5: z = 0. The homomorphism ϕ − a1 c : G → F5 is trivial on Gv and on Gv′ . So it is
unramified at the primes above 5. It is unramified at all other primes, by the definition of G. The
field E has no real place. So the extension of E cut out by ϕ − a1 c is abelian and unramified
everywhere. The class number of Q(i) is 1, so this extension is E and ϕ = a1 c. By Step 2 the image
of c in H 1 (G, W ) is 0. Hence z = 0.
    Step 6: the whole group. A continuous cocycle G → W has finite image, because G is compact
and W is discrete. So its values lie in the subgroup killed by some power of 5, and that power kills
its class. If z ̸= 0 were a class in Sha5 (E, 4) of order 5k , then 5k−1 z would be a nonzero class in
Sha5 (E, 4) killed by 5. Step 5 excludes this. So Sha5 (Q(i), 4) = 0.
    The congruences of Step 4 were recomputed by a script; the element 1 + 2i in place of 2 + i
gives the same relation. The result agrees with Corollary (b): if the description of the two parts
given above is granted, the part with ȷ = +1 is Sha5 (Q, 4), which is 0 because (5 − 1) | 2 · 4.
     Why the Bernoulli number is divisible by 5 and the group is 0 (from memory; not proved
here and not used). By the duality of Poitou and Tate, Sha5 (E, 4) is dual to the kernel of
H 2 (G, Z5 (5)) → v|5 H 2 (Gv , Z5 (5)), and by Tate’s local duality and Step 1 each local group has
                 Q

order 5. A consequence of the main conjecture [MW84] should give the order 5 v5 (B5,χ4 /5) = 5 for


                                                    158
the part of H 2 (G, Z5 (5)) with ȷ = −1. So the Bernoulli number would measure a global group
that maps injectively to the local groups. We have not compared this specialisation of the main
conjecture with [MW84]. The proof above does not use it. The class number of Q(ζ20 ), which is 1
(from memory), does not give the result by the method of Proposition G3: two primes of Q(ζ20 ) lie
above 5 and both ramify in the cyclotomic Z5 -tower, so [Was82, Prop. 13.22] does not apply.
    Three tests of the normalisations of the source, for the characters of conductor 1, 4, 8 and r ≤ 42:
Br,χ ̸= 0 exactly when χ(−1) = (−1)r (except χ = 1, r = 1, where B1,1 = 12 in the normalisation
above); the number N p≤r+1 p · Br,χ is an integer for N ∈ {fχ , 8} (and N Br,χ alone is not, in 42
                        Q

cases); and its absolute value is at most 16N r+1 r! (the largest ratio found is 1/32). For 1 ≤ e ≤ 40
the sum ℓ vℓ log ℓ over the odd primes in the numerators above is at most 2 log 16 · 4e+2 (e + 1)! ,
         P                                                                                            

the bound that PrimeBound gives for Q(i).
    Sizes. For the field of Theorem G the bound for the minus part in the table is 3√· 4n · t2 log(tN )
with n ≤ 2x2+8ε and t ≤ x401 : a power of x, about x804 . This upper bound is below b = exp(xε /2)
only for very large x: for ε = 1/53, from about log x = 742 on (script). The crossing illustrates a
sufficient scale, obtained from an upper bound. It gives no lower bound on the scale at which the
actual cohomology satisfies Theorem G.


6.   Assumed here, from the source

  (i) Theorem G. The statement VWin (ExistsPageN.lean:49). It is proved by vwin_of_row
      from Row0b, and Row0b by galoisLocalGlobalStrong_recParameter. Both are among the
      declarations the final theorem depends on, and vwin_of_row depends only on the three
      standard axioms.
 (ii) The inequality (III.257). The statement LinkN. Its proof in the source is in the theorems
      final_alt_alt and linkN_of_twGood_galoisPreconditions. Only the statements were
      read. The proof names bounds at the primes 2 and 3 (boundAtTwoThree_linked_of_recT)
      and a family of “Galois preconditions”.
(iii) The fact that dj = D0 (SmallLevelRankEq). The meaning of ηj (w) is not in this list of things
      taken from the source, because it is not established: the lattice definition (etaW, locLen,
      eta_units) is for the number of a family, and the declarations TieEta and etaN_eq_etaJ,
      cited earlier for the tie with the field etaJ of the record, are not among those the final theorem
      depends on (§2). The identification of ηj (w) with the defect of the index of lattices of Part II,
      §6.8 is open. Nothing proved here uses it.
 (iv) The identification of the data. That the numbers shaLen in the record of the construction are
      the lengths hℓ,e of the field E (nd.sha_eq); and the passage from Theorem G to HypV described
      in §1: the change of scale (winPairs), the bound for the number of twists (RankVal and the
      theorem eventually_rank_le_rpow_of_lt_one_div_fiftytwo) and supplyOfAdmOf. The
      statements and the proof of winPairs were read; the other proofs were not.
 (v) Every row of the table of §4, the hypotheses of the row for Case I, and the inputs listed after
     it.

    What this section proves, and what it assumes. Proved here outright: Lemma G1 and
Proposition G2. Proved here from theorems of [Was82], checked in the first edition (Propositions
6.16 and 13.22, Lemma 13.16, Theorem 6.17, Corollary 5.14): Proposition G3 and the corollaries (a),
(b), (c); corollary (b) also uses Lemma G4. Proposition 13.22 does not state the compatibility with
Π; the proof of Proposition G3 supplies it. The divisibility of Be+1,χ4 /(e + 1) by 5 for e ≡ 0 (mod 4)

                                                  159
follows from Theorem 5.11 and Corollary 5.13 of the same book. Proved here from Minkowski’s
lattice point theorem ([Neu99], Ch. I, (4.4) with its strict inequality, and (5.2); checked in a copy):
Lemma G4. Proved here from the standard statements of local and global class field theory (the
reciprocity law for principal ideles, the action of the local reciprocity map of Q5 on roots of unity,
and the triviality of the Hilbert class field of Q(i)), for this single field and twist: Sha5 (Q(i), 4) = 0.
Not proved here: the description of the two parts of Shaℓ (Q(i), e) by characters; the remark on the
order of H 2 (GE,5 , Z5 (5)) after the proof for Q(i); the two identifications with the Lean stated in
§5; and the meaning of ηj (w) as a length of a quotient of lattices (§2). Computed exactly: the table
of §5 and the three tests. Taken from the source: Theorem G, (III.257), items (i)–(v). Cited as
published theorems: the correspondences in the last column of the table of §4, and the theorems of
Soulé, Voevodsky and Rost, Mazur and Wiles, and Kurihara quoted in §3. They are cited from
memory, except where a theorem or a section is named; a named place in an article was compared
by a reviewer, not by us (see “Citations” at the head of the section).
    What is in the literature and what is not. We sort the statements of the source named in
this section into three lists. The source proves all of them again.
    (A) Published, and cited with the place. FerreroWashington: [FW79] (from memory), in the
form with bounded rank by [Was82, Prop. 13.23] (read). The duality theorems in LitCoh: Poitou
and Tate [NSW00, §8.6]; for Tate’s local theorems, the formula of Greenberg and Wiles and the
local theorems of Bloch and Kato the attribution is from memory. BhattScholzeIntegral: [BS23,
Thms. 1.2 and 5.6] (compared by a reviewer). We did not compare the Lean statements with the
published ones word by word. PropZ does not belong to this list: it is not cited from a publication.
Its classical form is Corollary (b), proved in §5 from class numbers; that Corollary (b) is PropZ
depends on the identification of Chr.V stated in §5, which was not checked in full.
    (B) A published counterpart exists, and it covers less or has not been matched. PsiBound:
an upper bound for a cohomology length in terms of the valuation of a Bernoulli number with
explicit integral factors. It corresponds to a consequence of [MW84] (from memory); general
bounds from Euler systems are in [Rub00, §2.2], and the specialisation is not supplied. Row0b: a
continuous semisimple representation, with predicted weights and the local comparisons in PropsB;
compare [CH13], [Car12], [Car14, Thm. 1.1], with normalisations and hypotheses still to be matched.
KatoTwoW: the two rational Selmer spaces of Bloch and Kato vanish under the attachment, Used
and WitAQ hypotheses; Kato [Kat04, §14.1, Thm. 14.2(1)] proves finiteness of the discrete Selmer
groups at the critical twists other than the central one. LemmaK: Soulé gives finiteness ([Sou79], from
memory) and a quantitative bound away from small primes ([Sou03, Thms. 2 and 3], compared by
a reviewer). We have not identified a published estimate for LemmaK in its full small-prime range.
OneCharU_s: the method of congruences is illustrated by [BC04, Thm. 1.1], which constructs a
Selmer extension; the upper bound is in list (C).
    (C) Appears to be new, or not identified in the literature. Theorem G itself (VWin); the
bounds for the plus part, CountI, OneCharU_s, PropGK, CountII, CountIII, CountV, SumIn,
SumOut; the uniform statement ABound with the window, ThmBab; LinkN; the statement
SymplecticFourImageDense on GSp4 ; and LemmaK in its range of small primes. Each of them is a
theorem of the archive that the final theorem depends on. Three of these names are not explained
in the body. SumOut says: for the odd primes ℓ ∈  / B together, the sum of the plus parts over E,
weighted by log ℓ, for the twists e < t, is at most (N t)C , with the level N of the source. PropGK
says: there is a nonzero integer, of logarithm bounded by a power of N (e + 1) times log p for an
auxiliary prime p (and a factor that depends on the orbit), such that for every ℓ =    ̸ p twice its
ℓ-adic valuation bounds the sum of the normalised lengths over an orbit of characters. CountV is
described after the table of §4. For ThmBab and SumIn the table of §4 names related work (Ferrero


                                                   160
and Washington, Iwasawa, Soulé); that work is not a counterpart of the statements. An upper
bound for the plus part of the groups Shaℓ (E, e), e ≥ 1 (groups of twisted Galois cohomology; they
are not ideal class groups), by a congruence number of automorphic forms, uniform in the field and
the twist, is not a published theorem as far as we know. Every statement of novelty in this list is
provisional: it is a judgment made without a search of the literature, and it was not tested.
   What remains.
  (i) To read the proof of one row of the table completely. The minus part (PsiBound) is the
      natural first choice: it concerns only abelian fields and Bernoulli numbers.
 (ii) To state OneCharU_s with its hypotheses on ρ (Adm3U_s, Adm2A) in classical terms, to compare
      them with Adm3 and Adm2, and to find how the nonvanishing integer of PropGK is produced.
(iii) To identify the published counterpart of the rows for GSp4 .
(iv) To establish the meaning of ηj (w): to find in the proof of LinkN (linkN_of_twGood_
     galoisPreconditions) the statement that ties the field etaJ of the record to the lattice
     length etaW at the twisted primes, and to check that the final theorem depends on it. The
     declarations TieEta and etaN_eq_etaJ do not serve: the final theorem does not depend on
     them. Until then the identification of ηj (w) with the defect of the index of lattices of Part II,
     §6.8 is not established.
                                                                       √
 (v) To check the constants in the passage from the three parts to b (vwin_of_joint), and to
     evaluate the constant cψ of §1 for the trivial character.
(vi) To prove the description of the parts of Shaℓ (Q(i), e) by characters, and to compare the
     remark on H 2 (GE,5 , Z5 (5)) in §5 with [MW84].
(vii) To state PsiBound over Q by descent, and to match the normalisations of Row0b with
      [CH13, Car12, Car14].


Computations
The numerical statements in this paper were computed with the following scripts (Python, with
mpmath and sympy); each prints the numbers quoted.

  e1_running_example.py                G, Gχ , Rq,d , the table for q = 3, d = 23, P and M (Part I, §I.1,
                                       I.5, I.6; Part II, §4.1–4.3, §5.2–5.3, §6.7)
  e1b_check_cells.py                   the eight cell constants, by a second method (Part II, §5.2)
  e2_lemniscate.py, e2b_size.py        the numbers An : divisibility and size (Part I, §I.3; Part II, §6.3)
  e4_sigma.py                          the Weierstrass σ-function: Gaussian size without the divisibil-
                                       ity (Part II, §6.5)
  e5_kronecker.py                      the two-variable function σ(z + w)/(σ(z)σ(w)) of the lemnis-
                                       catic lattice: divisibility at inert primes and size of its mixed
                                       derivatives (an experiment; its numbers are not quoted in the
                                       text)
  e6_hecke_theta.py                    Tn , the period, the rational numbers rk (Part I, §I.4.1)
